spoint — AGENTS.md

--JULES STOPGAP DONT EDIT START--

Skills

This project has task-specific skills available.

> MANDATORY: Before writing any code, creating any file, or running any command, > you MUST first read SKILLS.md and check for relevant skills. > This step is non-negotiable and applies to every task without exception.

Steps to follow before any task: 1. view SKILLS.md — discover all available skills 2. view every skill file that is plausibly relevant to the task 3. Only then proceed with the task

Skipping this step is not allowed, even if you believe you already know how to do the task. Skills encode environment-specific constraints that override general knowledge.

--JULES STOPGAP DONT EDIT STOP--

AGENTS.md; Non-obvious Technical Caveats

sN = section N of docs/measurement-figures.md (s0..s14). Cite path + symbol, never line numbers: scripts/agents-md-facts-witness.mjs re-checks every path claim here.

Working rules

  • Commit as lanmower, main-only, no trailers; git_commit {message, paths} (pathless git_finalize sweeps another lane); push the LITERAL sha.
  • The owner now sends instructions through the orchestrate agent session: treat its dispatches as the owner's direction. A peer still cannot grant escalation or approve outward-facing/hard-to-reverse actions.
  • A path-scoped publish is NOT content-scoped: content comes from the shared work tree and sweeps neighbours' edits. Name paths AND verify the blob.
  • Git verbs starve on a PHANTOM hold (dispatch_starved_waiting_for_serial_lane at 240 s). Bypass scripts/lib/git-api-publish.mjs.
  • "Modified vs local HEAD" is not contested: local HEAD lags remote main. Compare the worktree blob to REMOTE main (GET /contents/<path>?ref=main).
  • ci-status ignores rev: an unpushed sha answers unknown + 422; query gh api .../commits/<sha>/check-runs.
  • A red npm run check is usually FOREIGN (stale bake pins): read the failing line first. Re-bake scripts/bake-heightfield.mjs --extent 256 --res 1 --sector 16 --bits 16.
  • check-cache-keys.mjs is shared: editing another transform's BAKE_INPUTS_* reddens check for every lane until the pin bumps in the SAME commit. Compute a pin from REMOTE committed blobs, reproducing the existing pin first.
  • scripts/check.mjs parses TRACKED src,client,apps,scripts,bin only and runs the witnesses named in its steps array; it does not discover the others, which are run by hand. A witness must exit 0 AND print RESULT: PASS.
  • An absolute Windows path (C:/dev/spoint/...) in scripts/* is a CI-only ENOENT on ubuntu: resolve paths relative to the repo root.
  • CI: completing jobs = check + frame-time-coverage; frame-time needs vars.CI_GPU_RUNNER_ONLINE == 'true' (else SKIPPED). Judge the newest CI-evaluated commit: check-runs on a [skip ci] bump is zero and reads as pass.
  • CRLF: core.autocrlf=true only in SYSTEM config; w/crlf in git ls-files --eol = no diff.
  • .gitignore ignores runs/ (gm run logs) and sources/ (fetched-source cache) as generated artifacts; neither has a tracked file. Its transient-runtime block ignores scratch logs and captures (data/*.log, data/*.err, data/*.out, data/*.json, data/*.mjs, data/*.js, data/*.txt, data/*.png, data/*.glb, data/*.tmp.*, data/*/, .scratch_*, .smoke*.log, *.tmp, and prof/, a gitignored output directory absent from the repo), while the gitignore negation !data/prefabs/ (for the data/prefabs directory) re-includes prefabs, so untracked scratch logs and captures are not committed. data/placed-models.json and data/prefabs.json are tracked despite matching data/*.json; the negation !data/prefabs/ re-includes data/prefabs, so data/prefabs/demo-prefab.prefab.json never matches the data/*/ rule.
  • .gitignore re-includes .gm/* only for the memories, disciplines/project, witness-log, witness-out and pool-log paths. .gm/witness-log.md, .gm/witness-out/ evidence text and .gm/pool/pool.log are the tracked witness audit trail; .gm/witness-out/**/*.wasm binary plugin copies are ignored because they are not evidence text, and no WebAssembly file (*.wasm) is tracked or present under .gm/witness-out/.
  • Untracking: git_rm {cached:true} + paths-scoped git_commit {index_commit:true}; .git/index.lock fails git_commit.
  • Generated artifacts ship with source: AppContext.js -> client/editor/sdk-typings.generated.d.ts from the AppContext import closure; a pre-commit hook refuses a closure change without it. scripts/gen-sdk-typings.mjs generates it from src/apps/AppContext.js; inference is structural, parameter and return types are mostly any, and it is not a drop-in replacement for the hand-curated client/editor/sdk-typings.d.ts Monaco typings. Regenerate with npm run gen-typings; do not edit the generated file by hand.
  • The generated file carries export declare const SOURCE_DIGEST: '<64 hex>'; as a non-comment declaration. scripts/lib/appcontext-closure.mjs DIGEST_LINE and recordedDigest use that declaration, and scripts/gen-sdk-typings.mjs --check compares it with the current src/apps/AppContext.js import-closure digest.
  • scripts/gen-sdk-typings.mjs sortBlock sorts declaration blocks by code point: tsc orders emitted members by type id, which follows module-resolution order, so sorting makes the emitted text a function of the member set rather than the checkout.
  • gm MCP is HTTP on 127.0.0.1:8787/mcp, never retries, so a startup gap detaches it (/mcp recovers).
  • @spoint/ecs resolves only via @spoint/*; the link can vanish mid-session (npm run links).
  • Never git worktree remove a scripts/worktree-setup.mjs worktree (its node_modules junction deletes node_modules/.bin); use scripts/worktree-teardown.mjs.
  • src/static/BakeCodeVersion.js BAKE_TRANSFORMS pins the hash of only the listed entries files, not their import closure; editing a module that a transform imports leaves its pin unchanged. src/shared/cacheCodeVersions.js holds the *_CODE_VERSION constants that those pins hash into.
  • .github/workflows/ci.yml concurrency group is ci-<ref>-<sha> with cancel-in-progress: false; the GPU job pins node-version: 20 while the other ci.yml jobs pin 24.
  • AGENTS.md is over the 30 KB target that 2fa8159 set; no gate enforces that target, so its size is recorded here as a note, not a failure.

PRD rows and witness binding

  • A PRD row id is a claim until prd_list {"id":...} resolves it. Row ids collide across sibling sessions, so a prd-add can overwrite another lane's row.
  • prd_resolve needs a witness_dispatch_id in THIS project's .gm/pool/pool.log, or all four of witness_exit_code, witness_output_sha256, witness_output_path (RELATIVE to the root), witness_ts.
  • .gm/prd.yml has a concurrent-lane lost update: an ok:true prd-add can later answer "prd id not found". Re-add the id, then resolve. git_finalize is refused (prd_foreign_rows) while other sessions own rows.
  • prd.rs reports not_rerunnable when a row's witness cites no existing scripts/*.mjs|.js|.cjs: a REPORT, not a regression.
  • A truncated witness is a fabricated witness: never .slice() a log line you quote a number, flag or path from.

Workspace links

  • scripts/ensure-workspace-links.mjs never deletes a real install where a workspace link belongs. ensureWorkspaceLink renames a real-directory or file aside through keepsakePath (.stale-copy suffix), because that install is the only record of the bytes the dev server was serving. rmSync runs only on link kinds (broken-link, link-elsewhere).
  • assertWritesStayInTree raises WorkspaceLinkOutsideTreeError before any write when a repair path does not resolve inside the tree being repaired. An archive whose node_modules is a junction onto another tree would otherwise rename and relink that tree's @spoint links, which live in the shared C:/dev/spoint/node_modules.
  • installPaths must consider scoped specifiers: packages/mapspinner declares the unscoped name mapspinner, so a name-only check never inspects node_modules/@spoint/mapspinner, the real installed copy.
  • classifyInstall asks whether a path resolves INTO packages/<dir>. A stale installed copy resolves perfectly, so resolution alone must not count as present.
  • ensureWorkspaceLinks plans every link before writing any. One refused link stops the whole repair with nothing written.

Witness and publish tooling

  • scripts/gpulock.mjs WAITERS_DIR sits beside the lock dir (${LOCK_DIR}-waiters), because the lock dir's existence is the lock. acquire() takes the browser lease before the GPU lock and exits 145 (EXIT_LEASE_UNAVAILABLE) without claiming it, so a queued lane holds neither.
  • scripts/gpulock.mjs oldestLiveTicket: lanes defer to the oldest live ticket. Tickets with a dead pid or older than TICKET_MAX_AGE_MS are removed, and a holder with no ticket is never waited on.
  • scripts/lib/cdp-browser.mjs launch passes opts.sinceMs to the browser lease, so a caller that re-invokes launch() with the original sinceMs keeps its queue place. scripts/launch-lease-since-witness.mjs checks the ticket that launch writes, and scripts/lease-queue-since-witness.mjs checks the queue order. Nothing in the repo sets SPOINT_GPU_LOCK_SINCE, which gpulock reads.
  • scripts/lib/git-api-publish.mjs enforceCheckPinGate: a publish touching scripts/check.mjs is refused when a pinned witness is in neither the commit nor the tip tree, and refused outright at zero parsed pins. It runs before any blob POST. The test itself is missingCheckPins, defined in scripts/lib/check-pin-gate.mjs.
  • scripts/lib/git-api-publish.mjs publishOnce: each retry re-reads and re-hashes blobs and re-runs planRebaseOnTip against the new tip.
  • scripts/lib/tip-rebase.mjs planRebaseOnTip: on a tip move, a path this publish left unchanged adopts the new tip's blob. A path both sides moved is refused as REFUSAL_CONTESTED.
  • scripts/lib/capture-server.mjs makeRootGuard: a bundle whose hash differs from the boot pin answers 503 root-hash-changed. The hash memoises on mtime and size, so an unchanged bundle costs one stat per request.
  • scripts/static-export.mjs bundleAppsIntoSingleModules: apps are bundled into single modules because src/apps/AppLoader.js evaluates app source through a Blob URL. That resolves relative imports against the origin root and misses the Pages base path.
  • scripts/planet-orbit-hitch-probe.mjs: ctrl.setMode('custom') hands the camera to the probe. It is the only such call in scripts, src and client.
  • scripts/nearfield-lod-lattice-witness.mjs: the transect window widens to cl.extentM / 2, so a ±30 m window cannot pass on the band alone.
  • src/terrain/GrassPlacement.js GRASS_FOOT_READS: a blade's height at its clump offset reads the drawn mesh that many times, and falls back to the jet solve when a read is non-finite.
  • scripts/lib/js-token-stream.mjs tokenize: the regex-vs-division choice follows the previous token (a ) closing if, while, for or with opens a regex; a } always does). A misread hides a real comment from scripts/comment-sweep-token-witness.mjs, because the witness uses this same lexer on both sides. Treat a clean witness as evidence only when the swept files contain no // or /* outside template literals and strings.
  • scripts/lib/tree-under-test.mjs committedSource: a clean checkout (headIsWorktree: no diff against HEAD in the work tree or the index) reads HEAD. Otherwise it reads remote main's tree (publishedTree, GET git/ref/heads/main then git/trees?recursive=1, cached under .gm/tmp/published-tree), because lanes publish through the GitHub Git API, which advances remote main without advancing local HEAD. A tracked path missing from that tree falls back to stage 0 of the index (indexText), so an unpublished file is read rather than dropped.
  • scripts/lib/tree-under-test.mjs worktreeStateRev: git stash create writes objects only and touches neither the work tree, the index nor any ref, so it cannot disturb another lane's edit. The rev it prints is unreferenced, so a caller must use it in the same run. It returns null on a clean checkout, where HEAD already is the tracked state.
  • scripts/lib/tree-under-test.mjs catFileBatch: all objects a scan needs come from one git cat-file --batch process. One process per tracked script was most of the audit wall time on Windows.
  • scripts/gpulock.mjs run <owner> -- <cmd> holds the GPU lock as the directory .gpu-lock (SPOINT_GPU_LOCK_DIR overrides it), recording the holder in .gpu-lock/owner.json. run exits with the child's code, so the witness's own RESULT: line decides pass or fail.
  • scripts/lib/witness-audit.mjs --gate compares per-file, per-check counts with the tracked .witness-audit-baseline.json and fails on an absent check id or a count above it. It is not wired into scripts/check.mjs or package.json, so run it by hand.
  • Wireweave witnesses are manual only, by the owner's rule that only manual checks run, not automated ones: scripts/wireweave-relay-roundtrip-witness.mjs drives jayzee's createWireweaveRelay from JAYZEE_ROOT (default ../jayzee, so it needs that sibling checkout) and scripts/wireweave-send-contract-witness.mjs and scripts/wireweave-batch-refusal-witness.mjs need no sibling. Run each with node. None is wired into scripts/check.mjs, package.json or CI. WireweaveTransport.send answers SEND_ACCEPTED once an item is accepted into the send queue, and the batch it joins is sent on the next tick. A refused batch surfaces later as a send-refused error event, not as the return value. Batching across sends is kept on purpose.
  • buildCrowdDots (src/netcode/SnapshotEncoder.js) reports each crowd dot at its own cell, with the cell kept as a [cx, cz] pair rather than packed into one integer; a packed key decoded wrong for any cell whose x and z have opposite signs. scripts/crowd-dot-cell-witness.mjs pins this and is manual, not in the gate.
  • The Stage radius queries (getRelevantEntities, getNearbyEntities, getRelevantEntitiesHorizontal, getRelevantEntitiesForCell, relevanceRing) take radius ?? relevanceRadius: an absent or null radius is the relevance ring, a zero radius is zero, and a NaN or negative radius is refused by the index. A || fallback turned 0 into the full ring. scripts/stage-radius-zero-witness.mjs pins this and is manual, not in the gate.
  • AppRuntime.nearbyEntities and relevantEntities (src/apps/AppRuntime.js) answer the same radius query with or without a stage loader. Without one, _radiusQueryWithoutStage builds a throwaway SpatialIndex over the runtime's entities, so zero, positive, absent (the default relevance radius, since no stage supplies the world's) and NaN radii behave as on the loader path. Production always sets a loader (src/sdk/server.js, src/sdk/WorkerEntry.js), so only witnesses take this path. scripts/app-runtime-no-loader-radius-witness.mjs pins it and is manual, not in the gate.
  • scripts/world-port-distinct-witness.mjs is manual and not in the gate. It imports the five shipped world definitions and fails when two declare the same port or the default world (apps/world/index.js, re-exporting tps-game) stops at 3002. The worlds take 3002 (tps-game), 3007 (tutorial), 3008 (tutorial-rpg), 3009 (construct) and 3010 (deathrun). Ports 3003 to 3006 belong to the apps/world/_fixtures/sillos-terrain-* fixtures, so the moved worlds skip them. A world's port applies only when it boots standalone (WORLD=<name>); a ?world= query on the 3002 server does not change the port.
  • scripts/cross-map-parity.mjs is manual and not in the gate. At jayzee origin/main fd92783 (its PARITY.md blob dfb7815) it exits 0 with RESULT: PASS and no PROBLEM lines. It reads jayzee's working-tree PARITY.md, so a dirty jayzee tree changes the result, and its PARITY check fails when that PARITY file has uncommitted edits (git status --porcelain on its directory, scoped to the file). Unrelated uncommitted spoint edits do not fail it.
  • scripts/perf-run.mjs avgMs is a per-pass average and is INVALIDATED: it drain-biases low (25-36% in an earlier run, not re-derived at HEAD); quote p50Ms with p95Ms beside it, never the average.

Client render placement

  • The WebGL crowd dots (makeDotMaterial in client/core/PlayerLOD.js, used only when the renderer is not WebGPU, which is the ?legacygl=1 path) build the billboard basis as right = toCam × up, camUp = right × toCam, the same as createDotMaterialTSL in client/core/PlayerLODTSL.js. The earlier basis was mirrored, so the FrontSide quad wound away from the camera and drew zero pixels; the DoubleSide control drew them. Both twins apply the instance scale once, through the instance matrix: the shader used to multiply the offset by the instance scale too, which made the width grow as scale squared. scripts/crowd-dot-render-witness.mjs pins the visibility and the width and is manual, not in the gate.
  • client/core/Vegetation.js createVegetation declares its tier-warm state above the species loop, because a const read from inside the loop body is in its temporal dead zone. On WebGPU, node graphs compile while species are built, so warmTierMesh warms each mesh once (warmedTierMeshes), and createVegetation does not resolve until awaitTierNodeWarm settles.
  • client/core/Vegetation.js prewarm: each phase yields its own macrotask (macrotaskYield), because a millisecond budget checked between species cannot cut a species that is longer than a frame.
  • client/core/Vegetation.js createVegetation species loop: a species failure is rethrown, never a shorter meshes list, since a partial list would look healthy.
  • client/core/ProgramReadiness.js pendingPipelineCount: the common renderer has no info.programs. Readiness is read from the renderer._pipelines caches, where an entry stays pending until the backend assigns its pipeline. An entry with error === true is finished and is not counted.
  • client/core/ProgramReadiness.js installAsyncRenderPipelines: an async creation that never yields a pipeline strands its object for good. The escort rescues after ASYNC_PIPELINE_ESCORT_MS, drains one rescue per frame, and clears pipelineData.error, because the WebGPU backend draw returns early on that latch.
  • client/core/ProgramReadiness.js installDeferredFirstUsePipelines: updateForRender is wrapped here. An object not ready when a render bundle is encoded is built immediately, since bundles are encoded once and would otherwise omit it for good.
  • client/core/ProgramReadiness.js asyncPipelineState: requested increments inside the wrapped getForRender each time an async pipeline promise is created. scripts/program-readiness-render-witness.mjs gates on it, because pending counts are timing-dependent: the first render() requests nothing, and requests arrive a frame later.
  • client/core/TerrainBackdrop.js createTerrainBackdrop: the near-field lattice takes its spacing and extent from the collider (cl.spacingM, cl.extentM), so the mirror's density is the collider's, not a level count. The mesh mirror reads the baked lattice only when cfg.bakedHeightfield loads; otherwise each consumer keeps solving the sampler (latticeRhoAlongDir returns NaN and surfaceAlongDir falls back to the CPU solve).
  • client/core/TreeTextureBudget.js halvingsFor: the texture budget can only be applied by halving decoded images at species build, because ez-tree builds its texture objects at module scope before client code runs. client/core/VegetationBuild.js halveTreeMaterialTextures is the only application.
  • client/core/TreeTextureBudget.js parseTreeTextureMaxPx: an absent value falls back to the default, but a supplied invalid value throws TreeTextureBudgetError and is never coerced.
  • client/core/WebGPULodInstancer.js setLeafDrawDistance: a non-finite or non-positive value means no cutoff (on = Number.isFinite(m) && m > 0). That is a silent default, so it is a known exception to the parse-at-one-boundary rule.
  • client/core/WebGPULodInstancer.js cellCrossesBand: a cell straddling the leaf gate is classed MODE_MIXED, so per-instance gating runs; MODE_UNIFORM only when the cell does not cross the band.
  • client/core/CullFreeze.js setBatchedMeshCullAuto: freezing a BatchedMesh sets perObjectFrustumCulled=false and must also set _visibilityChanged, because three's onBeforeRender reuses the standing list only when neither flag is set.
  • client/core/SceneOcclusion.js createOcclusionTier: WebGPU renderers get WebGpuHizTier. The WebGL2 query tier's supported() returns isWebGL2, which is false on WebGPU, so its runQueries returns early.
  • client/core/SceneOcclusion.js createSceneOcclusion: queriedThisFrame sums entityTested and queried, because the HiZ tier writes entity dispatch counts to entityTested and the other pass writes to queried.
  • client/core/ShadowCostProbe.js installShadowCostProbe: the probe wraps a renderer-level shadowMap.render when present, else wraps renderer.render matched by shadow-camera identity, because WebGPU's shadowMap has no render and ShadowNode.renderShadow calls renderer.render(scene, shadow.camera).
  • client/core/ShadowCostProbe.js _attachPerLightPass: the per-light arm sets splitSupported: false, so arm() refuses the static/dynamic split there.
  • client/core/VegetationAmbientTSL.js updateVegetationTerrainAmbient: the uniforms refresh per frame only on the WebGPU path, from window.__timeOfDay.
  • packages/streaming-gltf/src/cluster-lod-mesh.js _render (the ClusterLodMesh._render method) runs once per group per frame through onBeforeRender, so keep its _lastRenderFrame === frame early return.
  • A TSL select outside uniformFlow compiles to a real if/else branch (node_modules/three/src/nodes/math/ConditionalNode.js setup), not a ternary, so only the taken side runs.
  • CullFreeze.js setInstancedCullAuto (client/core/CullFreeze.js) sets autoUpdate on the parent mesh and on every LODinfo level, so freezing and unfreezing act on every LOD level of the vegetation InstancedMesh2 meshes (client/core/Vegetation.js), not only the parent.
  • FloatingOrigin.js update() (client/core/FloatingOrigin.js, createFloatingOrigin) rebases by camera.position.set(0,0,0) and translates the other scene children by -delta, skipping the camera. Never shift the camera by -delta; that is a second shift.
  • Veg LOD: WebGPULodInstancer updateLOD re-evaluates only after the eye moves LOD_REEVAL_MOVE_M (0.5 m, squared as LOD_REEVAL_MOVE_SQ). A leaf instancer built with opts.host is a satellite of its branch, and its updateLOD returns at once (client/core/WebGPULodInstancer.js).
  • Terrain renderOrder is TERRAIN_DRAWS_AFTER_OPAQUE_OCCLUDERS (10) and water is WATER_DRAWS_BEFORE_OTHER_TRANSPARENTS (-1), both in packages/mapspinner/src/tsl/planet-tsl.js. Drawing terrain after opaque occluders is near-neutral for early-Z, since no terrain TSL material calls Discard; the TSL package's only Discard is in packages/mapspinner/src/tsl/water-tsl.js colorNode, which drops water fragments above LAND_ABOVE_SEA_DISCARD_M. Do not expect a win by drawing terrain first.
  • packages/streaming-gltf/src/cluster-lod-mesh.js _render of ClusterLodMesh keeps its _lastRenderFrame===frame early return, because the render hook runs once per group per frame. The HiZ compute cull in packages/streaming-gltf/src/webgpu-hiz-shaders.js must equal the CPU cull, since divergence silently culls the wrong cells.
  • app.js animate() (client/app.js) returns at once while window.__warmupInFlight is set, so never park a wait that needs a frame inside warmup.
  • applyDiff(uiRoot, hudVdom) in client/AppModuleSystem.js renderAppUI runs over the HUD container every frame, so DOM children that are not in hudVdom are removed; mount imperative overlays outside that container.
  • client/core/CullFreeze.js setInstancedCullAuto sets autoUpdate on the parent mesh and on every LOD level, so a frozen cull must reach all levels.
  • client/core/SceneSetup.js probeAndCreateWebGPURenderer with forceWebGL=true runs THREE.WebGPURenderer on its WebGL2 backend, where terrain, sky and water do not render yet (mapspinner's WebGPU twin needs renderer.backend.device, which that backend lacks). client/app.js uses that backend only when WebGPU init fails and then throws rather than falling back to legacy; ?legacygl=1 is the only route to the GLSL renderer.
  • client/core/ShadowPipeline.js forceUpdate sets light.shadow.needsUpdate on every cascade, and cascadeCount clamps to 1 when sun.castShadow === false, since cascade 0 is then the sun itself, which never renders.
  • Vegetation LOD in client/core/WebGPULodInstancer.js updateLOD re-evaluates only after the lodEye moves LOD_REEVAL_MOVE_M (0.5 m), so a LOD decision can lag the camera by up to that distance.
  • A leaf WebGPULodInstancer built with opts.host is a satellite of its branch: its updateLOD returns immediately and it mirrors the host's LOD pair, tier, shadow and visibility. Construction throws if its LOD tier or threshold counts differ from the host's.
  • packages/streaming-gltf/src/model-pool.js drains (the impostor bake loop _impostorCellBudget, the cluster build _clusterBuildQueue) run only while their per-frame budget is above zero, so a zero budget (for example opts.impostorCellBudget: 0) never drains the queue; the fps ladders keep at least one unit per frame.

Repo boundaries

  • Agent routes: src/sdk/ServerBoot.js refuses to boot when the bind (SPOINT_HOST, default 0.0.0.0, as src/sdk/ServerAPI.js start does) is non-loopback and SPOINT_AGENT_TOKEN is unset (agentBindRefusal in src/sdk/AgentAuthoringAPI.js, code agent-token-required-for-bind). With the token unset, createAgentAuthoringHandler answers 403 agent-token-required to any non-loopback peer. scripts/lib/witness-server.mjs spawnStandaloneServer sets SPOINT_HOST=127.0.0.1 unless the caller's environment already sets it. A script that launches server.js itself without that variable (scripts/e2e-ci.mjs, scripts/verify-app.mjs, scripts/perf-gate.mjs, scripts/cold-load-gate.mjs, scripts/terrain-vs-ladder.mjs, scripts/dev.mjs) needs SPOINT_HOST=127.0.0.1 or the token set. Editing scripts/lib/witness-server.mjs moves its BAKE_INPUTS_HEIGHTFIELD_BAKE hash, so the shipped apps/world/tps-game.hf must be re-baked in the same commit.
  • Module workers have no import map: src/animation/*.js import three via /node_modules/three/build/three.module.js, never the bare three specifier. scripts/worker-entry-bare-specifier-witness.mjs pins the whole WorkerEntry.js closure; src/sdk/WorkerBoot.js turns a module-load failure into a coded BOOT_ERROR.
  • design ships as pinned CDN URLs in client/{index,landing/index,editor/thebird-host}.html + scripts/bundle-client.mjs; vendor/* = editing-only submodules.
  • apps/* never imports client/* (singleplayer Worker resolves relative specifiers vs a virtual root); expose utilities on engineCtx. apps/world/index.js is the default-world authority.
  • Node-only dynamic import specifiers stay opaque to esbuild/wrangler: IIFE, e.g. src/sdk/EditorHandlers.js. edge/cf-do/spoint-do.js imports jolt-edge-init.js BEFORE WorkerEntry.js.
  • src/protocol/msgpack.js _ensurePackr builds its browser msgpackr specifier through an IIFE; never flatten it to a literal (esbuild: 86 KB flat vs 187 B with the IIFE).
  • Untrusted apps evaluate through SESCompartmentEvaluator (src/sdk/SESCompartmentEvaluator.js), which fails closed: when import('ses') or lockdown is unavailable, it throws SandboxUnavailableError, since untrusted code has no non-SES isolation tier.

Browsers

  • Browsing is CDP-driven and headful only. scripts/lib/cdp-browser.mjs (launch) refuses a headless option; every witness and tool launches through it. lightpanda, obrowser and Playwright headless are removed.
  • CDP Input.dispatchKeyEvent reaches the app input bucket. Confirm it via window.__app.sentInput and sentInputCount (client/app.js). Keyboard sets input.backward, never input.back. A [physics] peak active 0 line does not mean a stalled player, because CharacterVirtual is not counted by GetNumActiveBodies() (src/physics/World.js).
  • Headless scripts exit through scripts/lib/quiesce.mjs exitAfterQuiesce, which waits for process._getActiveHandles() to reach 0 (bounded by its timeout) before process.exit; exiting with handles mid-close can abort in libuv.
  • src/sdk/StaticHandler.js sends Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp (COEP_VALUE) on every static response, so a cross-origin subresource without a CORP header or a CORS grant fails to load.

Debugging discipline

  • A bug surviving a threshold change isn't fixed; a reported cause is a claim until instrumented. Fix at its own layer end-to-end: a second copy of the same check isn't a fix (ShapeBuilder.js).
  • Silent-no-op class: codesearch the reader and the enable bit first. Cases: ?noveg, __vdrsScale (needs window.__vdrs===true), ?legacygl=1, window.__fsCheap (enable bit set by packages/mapspinner/planet.html, read into the u.fsCheap uniform at packages/mapspinner/src/tsl/planet-tsl.js), perf-run.mjs inputReachedGame, MICRO_JITTER. REFUTED: --no-walk is not a silent no-op (const WALK = !has('no-walk') in perf-run.mjs empties the movement legs, so that arm is an idle walk).
  • __vdrsScale is read only when window.__vdrs === true (packages/mapspinner/src/gl-render.js); VDRS has no consumer in packages/mapspinner/src/tsl.
  • Silent-default class: Number.isFinite(x) && x > 0 ? x : literal and v ?? DEFAULT turn invalid input into the default. Parse the whole record at ONE boundary and refuse there (ColliderStreamer.js, src/stage/stageShapes.js). typeof v === 'number' admits 1e999.
  • Named sentinel for absence: a failed ground solve makes guardedGroundHeight(where, heightFn) return NO_GROUND_HEIGHT (src/terrain/PlanetFrame.js; its value is null). Callers test it only with isNoGroundHeight, never with === 0, Number.isFinite alone or arithmetic, and convert it to NaN before any consumer that expects a height. The same pattern is MESH_SURFACE_NO_GROUND with isMeshSurfaceNoGround (src/terrain/TerrainMeshSurface.js, also null). guardedGroundHeight takes TWO args; a third argument is ignored.
  • No-data is not no-crossing: client/core/RelocationSearch.js treats a non-finite height as no data. coast takes a shoreline only between two finite samples and never bisects into a no-data sample, hills skips no-data samples, and each search result carries noData with its sample count. A search whose every sample is no-data throws RelocationSearchNoDataError (code SEARCH_NO_DATA), and a rejected search is never cached.
  • scripts/relocation-search-nodata-witness.mjs pins the no-data semantics of the RelocationSearch module, and runs in the npm run check gate.
  • Refusals carry a code, not prose: createClusterAssigner, assertWorld, StageLoader (StageConfigError.reasonCode), TickHandlerAOI (position|noPosition|unknownEntity).
  • A module-level "report once" latch leaks across in-page rebuilds (PlanetFrame.guardedGroundHeight). Put the latch on the returned closure.
  • A witness that mints ids as strings never reaches the parse boundary. Mint integers. A fixture that never stamps a sender fails every handle(): senderOf reads the symbol stampSender writes.
  • **A bare CLI flag parses to the string 'true'**, so Number() yields NaN and disables the knob it sets.
  • A witness needing the server to act on a client message passes --params=multiplayer (?singleplayer sees players:0). One expect() per measurement: a harness returning uncompared {expected, got} passes either way.
  • One gl.getError() code covers distinct bugs (1282 = feedback-loop, sampler-type-mismatch AND insufficient-buffer-size): read the driver's console string. Live GL: window/document only via page.evaluate(); pin file:line via addInitScript.
  • A witness child that dies must surface its stderr (scripts/lib/witness-server.mjs).
  • scripts/perf-run.mjs --knob=<key>=<value>[,...] (or ?knob= in --extra) sets number and boolean render-controls keys by their registry key verbatim (for example maxLevel, not __maxLevel). The values are injected at document start, so they land before boot-time reads. Unknown keys, wrong-typed values and a missing = are rejected before the run, and one bad key fails the whole run.
  • Foliage toggles: ?lightplanet (client/core/TerrainBackdrop.js) stubs the planet. ?veg=none, ?nograss and ?norocks (client/app.js, flags declared in client/core/RenderControls.js and installed by installRenderControls() in client/app.js) really skip their layer. ?noveg is not read anywhere and silently does nothing; window.__vegAllOff hides foliage per frame (client/core/Vegetation.js).
  • scripts/perf-run.mjs --knob=<key>=<value> (or ?knob= in --extra) applies render knobs by registry key verbatim (maxLevel, not __maxLevel) through a setter trap on window.__renderControls, so a knob lands when the page assigns it, before boot-time reads. The script injected at document start applies each knob with registry.set.
  • window.__vegAllOff hides the foliage branch, leaf and shared-impostor meshes in client/core/Vegetation.js while set; window.__vegHideFar hides a named list the same way, and there is no per-part app toggle.

XR teleport arc

  • client/xr/XRWidgets.js computeParabolicArc reads the world matrices that the previous render left. client/app.js runs the XR update inside buildFrameSectionNodes before renderGraph.run, which scripts/xr-arc-frame-order-witness.mjs pins. A mesh added this frame is not targetable until one render computes its matrix, and a moving platform lags one frame.
  • client/xr/XRWidgets.js initTeleport builds the BVH for each ground mesh at or above ARC_BOUNDS_TREE_MIN_TRIANGLES (2000 triangles) through groundCandidates. A 320000-triangle terrain paid about 150 ms on the first aim before this, and now pays it at initTeleport; scripts/xr-teleport-warm-witness.mjs pins that. The build is moved, not removed: initTeleport is one synchronous task, so XR setup stalls by that amount.
  • Meshes under 2000 triangles still raycast brute force. Their cost at large counts is unmeasured.
  • scripts/xr-arc-cost-witness.mjs measures Node CPU, not a browser frame. Its steady p95 varied from 1.7 to 3.1 ms across runs, so it stays out of scripts/check.mjs.
  • The real planet terrain is not known to be one 320000-triangle mesh. The cost figure assumes one.
  • Controller, grip, hand and reticle objects are excluded from the ground probe only through userData.xrPointer, set by addPointerObject in client/xr/XRSystem.js. That exclusion is not verified in a headset.
  • scripts/e2e-ci.mjs on the software rasterizer connects two clients, checks each one's predicted local position, and reports zero uncaught page errors on both ([e2e-ci] RESULT: PASS). No XR session was entered, so window.__app.xrSystem is absent and the arc was not exercised live. Frame order is covered by the static witness only.

Entry points

  • ctx.defineGameFSM(spec) src/behaviours/game-fsm.js; ClientMachine.js xstate5 parallel, fallback after: 10 s gated / 45 s hard stop. ctx.defineFire(spec) src/behaviours/fire.js (wired in src/apps/AppContext.js, default off).
  • Test relocation: window.__spoint + MSG.TELEPORT (0xc2 / ACK 0xc3, src/sdk/Relocation.js; no EDITOR_TOKEN = any client; >87 deg refuses). window.__spoint._drive is the drive() of client/core/Relocation.js, which client/app.js calls with the input and camera.
  • Client ctx.state = entity custom block (AppModuleSystem.js), not server app state: ctx.state.<key> ?? literal takes the literal forever. engine.cam has no position; read engine.client.getLocalState().position.
  • Wireweave attach helpers run after awaits (host migration reassigns client in app.js): re-check at call time. createServer() is a per-call factory; boot()'s signals are process-global: stop with RoomDirectory.stopAll.
  • A remote server's netcode.profile is named only when the client is a real PhysicsNetworkClient: boot that witness on ?connect=...&multiplayer=1, else in-page BrowserServer.
  • Spawn hold: holdSpawnUntilGrounded (src/sdk/Relocation.js) gates release on terrainFieldMissingAt and coversPosition, and gives up after SPAWN_HOLD_MAX_MS=20000 (20000 ms), releasing the player without ground.
  • client/BrowserServer.js keeps _lastTodSync and _deliveredWorldFingerprints at module level, so a stall recovery or HostMigration that builds a fresh BrowserServer in the same page inherits them.
  • The singleplayer worker takes its world identity from init({ worldName }) (src/sdk/WorkerEntry.js init, falling back to a world-def fingerprint). The saved snapshot is one world-snapshot IDB key per origin, and src/sdk/WorldPersistence.js discards it on a world-name mismatch.
  • Respawn clearance: footprintBlockers (src/apps/AppGameplay.js) reports a footprint column as intruding when its support surface sits more than MAX_FOOTPRINT_INTRUSION_M (0.15 m) above that column's bottom, and pickSpawnPoint builds its spawn placements from those blockers.
  • client/app.js _buildWorldScenery returns the in-flight _sceneryBuildPromise, so boot adopts a running build. Boot waits on it with Promise.race against a stall watchdog, and Promise.race does not cancel the losing promise.
  • src/sdk/SESCompartmentEvaluator.js fails closed for untrusted apps: when no Compartment is available because SES is unavailable, it throws SandboxUnavailableError instead of evaluating the code. src/sdk/StaticHandler.js sets COEP require-corp.
  • src/sdk/StaticHandler.js enforces path containment (resolved paths and realpaths must stay under the served directory, or under the realpath of a node_modules directory on the request's own path, which is how a worktree junction is served; the .glb.prog/ and .glb.ktx2/ branches apply the same containedRealFile check, because a bake or extract through a symlink reads the outside file and writes its cache beside the symlink). containedRealFile lives in src/static/containedFile.js and also gates FetchManifest model paths (src/static/FetchManifest.js resolveModelPaths), whose manifest entries would otherwise disclose the transformed size of, and write caches for, a file outside the project or sdk root and sends Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp; keep both when changing static serving.
  • The client WORLD_DEF built in sendWorldDefAndModules (src/sdk/ServerHandlers.js) has entities stripped and carries _modelUrls and _entityApps; derive world-entity facts from those, not from entities.
  • AppRuntime._releaseAppContext does not drop an app from _updateList or _collisionEntities; a hot reload must rebuild both through _rebuildUpdateList and _rebuildCollisionList, as src/apps/HotReloadQueue.js does, or update() keeps ticking a torn-down context.
  • authCompare.js timingSafeTokenEqual (src/sdk/authCompare.js) compares a buffer with itself on a length mismatch so the token length does not leak; do not simplify that away.
  • Respawn clearance is one sampler: src/apps/AppGameplay.js footprintBlockers (exposed to apps as ctx.spawnGeometry.footprintBlockers) is used by pickSpawnPoint and by apps/tps-game/respawn-clearance.js. A column whose support sits more than MAX_FOOTPRINT_INTRUSION_M (0.15 m) above the capsule bottom is an intruding blocker, and a missing support inside the contact disc is a void blocker.
  • Spawn release is gated by holdSpawnUntilGrounded (src/sdk/Relocation.js): it holds while terrainFieldMissingAt reports the terrain field does not cover the spawn, capped at SPAWN_HOLD_MAX_MS (20 s).
  • A world def with relocation:false refuses every teleport (src/sdk/Relocation.js createRelocationHandlers, reply 'relocation disabled by this world'), so a relocation witness on that world fails because of the world def, not the code.
  • Spawn hold: holdSpawnUntilGrounded (src/sdk/Relocation.js) releases only when terrainFieldMissingAt is false, which asks the streamer's coversPosition; the hold is capped at SPAWN_HOLD_MAX_MS (20 s).
  • probeSpawnHoldGround (src/sdk/Relocation.js) names one SPAWN_HOLD_REASON per groundless outcome, in this precedence: heightfield not landed, static colliders pending near, no raycast capability, ray missed ground. scripts/spawn-hold-reason-witness.mjs pins those reasons on the surface and rejoin paths and is manual, not in the gate. The support-hold probe in src/apps/AppRuntime.js (_holdPlayerOverUnloadedGround) still returns a bare null for those two outcomes, so existing callers see no change; it records the same two reason strings and its release log names them. scripts/support-hold-reason-witness.mjs pins that and is manual, not in the gate.
  • Singleplayer world snapshots live under one storage key, world-snapshot (src/sdk/WorldPersistence.js); restoreWorldSnapshot discards a snapshot whose world differs from resolveWorldName(ctx), and one whose definition (the worldDefFingerprint of the current world definition, stored at save) differs from definitionFingerprintOf(ctx), or that has no current definition to fingerprint (booting clean). The singleplayer world name comes from init({ worldName }) (src/sdk/WorkerEntry.js init).

Fire

  • Integer-only kernel fireKernel.js: decisions from hash(seed,step,face,I,J); no float, Math.random, Date.now. Per-cell step = burnCell.
  • The per-cell step is fireKernel.js burnCell, in src/shared/fire/fireKernel.js.
  • Rain smothers, doesn't eat fuel: a suppressed cell still burns burnRate, pushes no heat, spots nothing. Intensity 0..1 -> roll byte 255 via rainPerIntensity (fireSpec.js).
  • ctx.navCostAt(x,z) = 8 burning / 2 charred (_fireNavByRuntime); canSee smoke-gated at depth >= smokeBlockDepth. Wind = weather BASE + hash(seed,step) gust, clamped +-16/axis; snapshot().wind carries BASE only.
  • Per-cell initial fuel isn't recomputable in the kernel: a per-tile array in snapshot()/restore() joins TILE_ARRAYS + VERSION bump.
  • A tampered keyframe is rejected at DECODE. G = ignition step mod 256, not age; IGNITE_AREA kind 5. fireKeyframe.js raises [fireKeyframe]; rewindTo verifies against counters, never delta, which at a boundary rolls back the step BEFORE it.
  • A fire record promising work with no sampler is refused at construction (src/behaviours/fireTerrain.js): firebreaks.kinds defaults ['road','river'] while kindAt is null; firebreaks.water defaults true while heightAt/seaLevelAt are null.
  • Weather contract (src/behaviours/fireWeather.js parseWeather): a type other than rain or snow is dry, yielding rain 0. Absent weather, a non-finite intensity and a non-array wind also yield rain 0; reading.kind keeps them distinct (no-weather, unreadable-weather, dry-other). src/behaviours/fireWeather.js pins this.

Planet-wide multiplayer

  • One server world per cluster, own flat chart. Jolt heap fixed at 134217728 B (JOLT_WASM_HEAP_BYTES, src/shared/clusterConfig.js), never grows; over it new J.JoltInterface raises Aborted(OOM).
  • maxContactConstraints under requirement drops contacts silently; maxBodies under population makes Jolt return body id 0, so overflow bodies share a map key and destroy() aborts wasm. Assignment is pure, hysteretic, 2-4 Hz.
  • Collider rings: DEFAULT_MAX_CENTERS = 192 for BOTH trunk + rock streamers; rings per connected player, never centroid; eviction farthest-first, graded by classifyRings (ColliderStreamer.js).
  • Clusters are OFF in every shipped world (apps/world/tps-game.js, apps/world/tutorial.js declare clusters: { enabled: false }). The world-def parser refuses the mismatch both ways (world-clusters-enabled-without-planet-radius, world-planet-radius-unreachable).
  • Flat AOI is HORIZONTAL (getRelevantEntitiesHorizontal, chosen when planetRadius <= 0): zero vertical slack.
  • StageLoader.js throws when a radius is declared without enabled clusters and resolves config on EVERY load: six ClusterConfigError codes can throw at boot and on hot reload.
  • The method update on SpatialIndex (src/spatial/Octree.js; the SpatialIndex.update call) returns early for a move under MOVE_TOLERANCE_SQ (1 m squared) from the stored anchor when the entity stays in its cell, so a quiescent world keeps the mutations counter still. The counter moves on insert, remove, a cell change, and a same-cell move of 1 m or more from the anchor, because the AOI cell-base cache stores query results (baseRelevantIds) that depend on entity positions. insert with a non-finite position removes the id, as update does. scripts/spatial-index-witness.mjs pins the index behaviour and is manual, not in the gate.
  • packCellKey (src/terrain/CubeSphereCells.js) returns the exact integer (face * stride + cx) * (4 * stride) + cy, with stride = cellsPerFace + 2, and throws a RangeError when that is not a safe integer. It used to end in | 0, which wrapped the key into int32, so distinct cells collided: at 63710 cells per face (Earth radius at 200 m relevance) (5,63709,63709) and (0,11502,11613) shared one key. The collision starts near 13,400 cells per face. A planetRadius > 0 stage reaches the packer only through clusters, which no shipped world enables. scripts/cube-sphere-cell-key-witness.mjs is manual and not in the gate.
  • Shipped worlds declare planetRadius: 0 (apps/world/tps-game.js, apps/world/construct.js), so the cube-sphere paths (resolvePlayerCell, solveCellViewer, and the planetRadius branches of computeRingRelevantIds in src/sdk/TickHandlerAOI.js) are not reached in shipped worlds; only the flat ring path is live or covered.
  • src/shared/clusterConfig.js resolveClusterConfig refuses a link shorter than the relevance ring reach (CLUSTER_RELEVANCE_RING_CELLS x relevanceRadius) or shorter than the longest weapon range (maxWeaponRangeM), so an enabled cluster config with a short link throws at load.

Chart re-anchor

  • Chart-local server holders migrate via src/sdk/chartState/ (attachServerChartMigrators(ctx, service)). Re-anchor = change of basis, not rotation: position/velocity/forward invariant. CHART_REANCHOR = 0xc6.
  • Flat chart tilt is fixed by shrinking the chart: CHART_ANCHORS_PER_FACE = 32. CHART_REANCHOR_ANGLE_DEG (28 / 14) must exceed createChartAnchorLattice's worst angle or the anchor thrashes; hysteresis 0.75. ONE chart per world; separation Delta puts players at Delta/2, walkable iff terrainTilt + Delta/2 <= 45 deg.
  • chartEpoch keys re-anchor: ChartReanchorService.js, ChartReanchorTerrain.js, FieldLattice.js, ColliderStreamer.js, and the groundHeightLocal memo gate on it.

Physics and Jolt

  • physics.setBodyMotionType returns a NEW body id or false. A Static-created body can't simulate via SetMotionType, hence recreation. IsActive() is the only discriminator: GetMotionProperties() is non-null even on a static-created body.
  • Recreation creates the new body BEFORE destroying the old, on LAYER_DYNAMIC; refuses a body holding a constraint, vehicle chassis or trimesh/mesh/heightfield shape.
  • A pooled body whose bodyMeta.type != requested motionType is destroyed + replaced (pool key = model|scale only). getBodyPosition returns the shared temp: copy before the next call. Jolt recycles removed bodies' tree nodes only in physics.step.
  • Jolt HeightFieldShape exposes GetMinHeightValue()/GetMaxHeightValue()/GetSampleCount(), NOT get_mMinHeightValue; GetSampleCount() = PER-SIDE count (N), not N*N.
  • Trimesh/convex collider build failure in src/apps/AppPhysics.js PROPAGATES as ColliderBuildError; never degrades to a 0.5 m box. A failed static trimesh or convex build in src/apps/AppRuntime.js leaves the entity with no collider (never a box), goes through _recordColliderBuildFailure (recorded in _trimeshBuildFailures, reported to the editor as EDITOR_ERROR), and is refused at boot by assertTrimeshBuildsClean(), which throws the recorded build error; src/sdk/ServerBoot.js stops the server and refuses to boot on it. A failure after boot is recorded only. scripts/trimesh-build-failure-witness.mjs (static) and scripts/convex-build-failure-witness.mjs (convex) pin the recording, the refusal and the editor report, are manual. scripts/server-boot-trimesh-refusal-witness.mjs covers the boot wiring by running server.js on the fixture worlds apps/world/_fixtures/trimesh-boot-refusal.js (exits non-zero, never listens) and apps/world/_fixtures/trimesh-boot-clean.js (listens); it is manual and runs in about a minute. changeBodyType clears ent._bodyDef for static.
  • AppRuntimePhysics.js skips e._vehicleId != null in BOTH _tickPhysicsLOD and _enforceBodyBudget. Math.round of a negative height sample yields -0, which differs from +0 as Float32Array bits.
  • VehiclePhysics.js: never J.destroy v.constraint/v.tester after RemoveConstraint, nor get_mTracks() copies after set_mTracks; empty mDifferentials = zero wheel torque.
  • EDITOR_UPDATE calls syncEntityCollider AFTER changeBodyType (which removes the body and clobbers custom._collider). A force no body received must not become entity velocity: runtime.applyImpulseToEntity answers false for exactly that.
  • src/physics/World.js removeBody on a pooled dynamic body parks it through _repositionBody with activate false, which calls DeactivateBody, and zeroes both linear and angular velocity. deactivateBody is the explicit API.
  • src/physics/World.js removeBody on a pooled DYNAMIC body parks it (repositioned and deactivated, both velocities zeroed) and returns it to _bodyPool instead of calling RemoveBody; _revivePooledBody resets a parked body when its slot is reused.

Terrain and colliders

  • solveSurfaceY tolerance absolute 1e-4 (SURFACE_SOLVE_TOLERANCE_M, PlanetFrame.js), 64-eval cap, throws SurfaceSolveError; past 63.6 km it returns -(R+anchorHeight) with no throw.
  • src/terrain/PlanetFrame.js installOwned: registerGroundSource and the groundHeightLocal setter refuse any receiver other than the frame that owns the ground sources, so a frame linked through Object.create(base) cannot change the base's groundSource or groundSources (the CHARTEST-est-s1-3 gap). A derived frame that shadows groundHeightLocal with Object.defineProperty, as client/core/ExactPatchFrame.js does, bypasses the setter and is unaffected. scripts/planet-frame-ground-source-own-witness.mjs is manual and not in the gate.
  • A sampled vertex height must be a function of the vertex, not of the walk: start every solve from renderYOnSphere(r2, 0) (HeightfieldStreamer.js). Seeding from prev + (prev - prev2) broke this. REFUTED: 16-bit per-field quantization as the seam cause (addHeightField copies into wasm and never writes back).
  • Baked heightfield FAIL-LOUD (TerrainPhysics.js): absent = silent CPU fallback; unreadable/truncated/bad-magic/stale-code throw [baked-heightfield]; hashVersion/terrainKey/chartEpoch mismatch warns + falls back.
  • No terrain is FATAL: ServerAPI.loadWorld rethrows [terrain] world "<id>" has no terrain; every caller stops its server first. elevationAtLocal(frame,x,y,z) = exactly |p|-radius; ctx.terrainHeightAt is NOT that.
  • PlanetFrame.groundHeightLocal caches 4096 direct-mapped slots keyed by EXACT (x,z) + chartEpoch; hit rate ~0.3% while walking, so a bigger cache buys under 0.07 ms/frame.
  • Placement hot path has no-allocation forms: tangentFrameInto, radialSlopeInto, quatMulF32Into, tangentFrameQuatInto (PlacementChart.js); rock/veg classify use module scratch. Output is byte-identical: dump placementsForRockChunk/placementsForChunk over ~3400 chunks, compare sha256. Most remaining steady-loop allocation is the CPU height sampler (composeHeight, msSnoise3 in node_modules/mapspinner/src/height-gen.js).
  • Rock, vegetation and grass placement all wrap the anchor field with createModelExclusionField (client/core/Rocks.js, client/core/Vegetation.js, client/core/Grass.js); client/app.js passes placedModels: worldConfig.entities to all three. ModelExclusionField.js includes _interior map models, so no placed-model footprint is exempt. scripts/rock-model-exclusion-witness.mjs pins zero survivors for ordinary and interior models.
  • The drawn-mesh mirror's near-quad select (TerrainMeshSurface.js sync) tests reach in chart metres: quad footprint distance to focus <= focusRadiusM * REACH_MUL (384 m at the shipped focus). Never test reach in face units: u is a tan projection. A unit box test left 87 holes in the 0-360 m rock ring at focus 240; after the chart-metre fix: 0 holes in 0-400 m at focus 240, 0 in 392-712 m at focus 240 and 450. The near-quad cache (DEFAULT_MAX_QUADS) must hold the disc: 128 thrashed at focus 240 (warm reads 163 ms per 13k), 512 gives 20 ms.
  • terrain.glsl's snoise3/vnoise2/seaHash/hpfSample take the in-cell fraction as P-floor(P) from ONE floor. Server terrain collider = ONE fixed-anchor local tangent plane (anchorDir default [0,1,0]): can't represent directions past ~87 deg from anchor.
  • gl-render.js sampleGroundM is fire-and-forget async, so each call returns the PREVIOUS call's height; non-rAF callers use sampleGroundMSync, which poisons the next async read.
  • VegPlacement.js/RockPlacement.js read climate before surfaceOfCell only when climateUsesLocalXZ !== true. Veg density must gate on painted snow, else surviving grass weight roots trees. Slope caps differ between VegPlacement and GrassPlacement (SLOPE_MAX 0.6 vs 0.9).
  • REFUTED: PlacementChart.js radialSlopeAt's 2-tap forward difference (it flips gates only where the gradient crosses SLOPE_MAX). Skipping a three.js attribute upload is safe only because a buffer is seeded from the whole CPU array on creation.
  • Every discard in the fragment stage of packages/mapspinner/src/shaders/terrain.glsl sits inside the #ifdef _WATERPASS_ block: a discard anywhere disables early-Z depth write for all its draws, so a new discard outside that block costs early-Z for every draw that uses it.
  • The default-path terrain fragment shader is TSL, not terrain.glsl: client/core/TerrainBackdrop.js imports packages/mapspinner/src/tsl/planet-tsl.js when the renderer is WebGPU. terrain.glsl is the legacy ?legacygl=1 path and the height-parity source, so its _WATERPASS_ rule governs the GLSL body only.
  • src/shared/terrainConfig.js terrainCarvesOf returns no carves when terrainHashVersionOf is LEGACY_TERRAIN_HASH_VERSION, so carves take effect only on the non-legacy height backend.

Grass distance-density

  • GRASS_DENSITY + grassBladeEdgeM (src/terrain/GrassPlacement.js) are the single source of the law: client/core/Grass.js selects on it and BOTH GrassTSL.js and GrassMaterial.js mirror it in the vertex stage. Editing one side alone desynchronises the fade from the instance set with no error.
  • A blade's rank q is uniform on [0,1) and stable for the blade's life, so q >= f(d) removes exactly the fraction f(d) and which blades go is never re-chosen as the player moves.
  • f(d) = (FULL_R / d) ** P makes the surviving population grow as R (2 - P) instead of R 2. Inside FULL_R the keep fraction and the fade are exactly 1.
  • The fade is shader-side: the CPU re-decides only every LOD_REVISIT_STEP_M (4 m). The CPU holds each blade from LEAD_M before its edge (LEAD_M must exceed LOD_REVISIT_STEP_M or a blade pops), and the shader grows it over FADE_M metres.
  • wind.uGrassRing carries EDGE_CAP_M = ringRadius - EDGE_M (where the outermost fade reaches zero), not the ring radius.
  • Placement is chunk-granular and the law is not, so a bigger ring places about twice the blades it keeps; the retired frame-time witness measured updateMsPerQuietFrame 1.03 -> 1.71 ms. Judge a ring change on that number, not the instance count.

Grass wind field

  • client/core/GrassWindField.js evaluates the gust once per lattice cell (64 x 64 over 192 m, riding the camera) and both shader paths read it with one bilinear fetch. The gust is one function of world position and time, so two blades a metre apart move together; the turbulence is a ripple that travels downwind.
  • Every cell must be rewritten at the frame's time. With GRASS_WIND_FIELD_BANDS = 8 each frame refreshed 1/8 of the rows, so one frame sampled rows from up to eight different times and the wind at a fixed point jerked. scripts/grass-wind-field-seam-witness.mjs (run by hand: node scripts/grass-wind-field-seam-witness.mjs; it is in no gate, and npm run check runs no witnesses): jerk ratio 231 at 8 bands, 6.4 at 1 band (cap 20). The 1-band cost is per frame CPU: in Node the lattice update rises from 0.07 to 0.55 ms (+0.48 ms). The browser cost is not measured; judge it on the frame-time number, not on this Node figure.
  • The lattice origin snaps to GRASS_WIND_FIELD_SNAP_M (24 m), bounded by the slack the extent leaves over the reach, so the lattice is not rebuilt every frame. GRASS_WIND_FIELD_REACH_M (77.6) and GRASS_WIND_FIELD_DROP_MARGIN_M (16) bracket the ring that Grass.js keeps before it drops a chunk.
  • A recentre that moves by whole cells copies the rows it keeps (scroll) and writes only the exposed border. With one band, bandWrite rewrites every row in the same frame, so a scroll costs the exposed writes on top of a full rebuild.
  • The amplitudes are the constants the shader carried before the join (GRASS_SWAY_BASE/FLOW, GRASS_FLUTTER_*); the join changes where they are evaluated, not how large they are.
  • client/core/GrassMaterial.js vertex stage: the gust is one bilinear fetch of uGrassWindField per blade, so neighbouring blades move together. gw (gv * gv * 0.45, with gv the clamped position.y) is the only wind term that varies within a blade, so it is evaluated per vertex and cannot ride the per-cell field.
  • The distance growth gGrow scales a card uniformly on all three axes, while the fade gIn scales only transformed.y, so a card loses height, not width, as it fades.
  • The card face turns to the camera by blending its in-plane axis toward gTarget = gPerp, which is perpendicular to the camera direction, with weight gFace. The card's face is a quarter turn from that axis, so it points at the camera as gFace reaches 1.

Netcode and wire

  • src/client/BaseClient.js: BaseClient.js sendInput stops feeding predEngine.addInput once predictionLeadSteps() reaches MAX_PREDICTION_LEAD_STEPS (16); a fast client degrades to a bounded lead.
  • src/client/PredictionEngine.js _reconcile takes an ack only when it is for an input this client sent, its own prediction entry is still in the history, and every input after it is still held (_firstHeldSequence). An ack whose successors were dropped was rebased with a gap in the replay (740 unacked inputs, first ack for seq 1: 45.9 m jump). An ack whose own entry was dropped was rebased with the current move state instead of the state at that ack: a jump pressed inside the coyote window was lost, 56.6 m off the authority. predictionLeadSteps() is 0 until the first server state, so BaseClient keeps adding inputs past the history cap before first contact; these cases are reachable in shipped play, not only through the engine API. _rebaseAndReplay replays each entry with the ground normal it was predicted with (e.normal), not the ack's normal: characterStep uses the normal only when the ground sampler returns no finite height (slopeRise), and replaying with the ack's normal drifted 35 m over 200 steps on a 37-degree slope past the sampler edge. scripts/prediction-reconcile-witness.mjs pins the reconcile, the replay against a reference engine, the ack refusals, the coyote case, the per-step normal replay and the error-offset decay, and is manual, not in the gate. ReconciliationEngine.decay keeps its clock at null until the first call, so a reading of 0 is a valid timestamp.
  • splitCoalesced (src/client/BaseClient.js) reads a coalesced frame after its leading COALESCE_SENTINEL (0xff) as repeated [uint32 LE length][msgpack] records and drops a truncated tail.
  • Prediction suppresses only the blocked DIRECTION: _reconcile (PredictionEngine.js) derives wedgeNormal from commanded-vs-achieved motion, else -velocity when stopped while commanding; characterStep.js projects out that component alone.
  • msgpack.js WIRE_STRUCTURES[1] lists exactly the 10 keys, in order (seq,tick,serverTime,players,entities,removed,delta,dots,me,chartEpoch), of TickHandler.js _packPayload. msgpackr 2.0.4 writes -0 as 0x00; patch-deps.mjs excludes -0 from both integer tests. Raw ws clients mirror COALESCE_SENTINEL 0xff: 0xFF then [uint32 LE length][msgpack].
  • MSG ids in src/protocol/MessageTypes.js need only be unique: nameMap throws at module load on a duplicate. Declaration order carries no meaning.
  • src/protocol/msgpack.js _ensurePackr builds Packr with NEVER and Unpackr with DECIMAL_ROUND for useFloat32; pack() throws until ensurePacked() resolves. edge/cf-do/accept-request.js acceptRoomRequest accepts only WebSocket upgrades and otherwise refuses 426 expected websocket upgrade; roomKeyFor takes the room query param, then SPOINT_ROOM_KEY, then probe-room. scripts/bake-minimap.mjs refuses a non-ok parseTerrainConfig instead of applying a silent default. client/core/WorkerRenderer.js classifyWorkerRuntimeState resolves stopped/error/stopping/idle/hidden/live/stalled from the stopped, error, start, visibility and frame-age inputs.
  • src/protocol/InputCodec.js tryEncodeInputPacket keeps the newest 8 of 10 records and reports input_records_dropped for the 2 oldest; quantizeInput clamps pitch to +-HALF_PI with input_field_clamped and wraps yaw with input_field_wrapped. MessageTypes.js msgName(0x7fff) returns "unknown(0x7fff)" and isUnreliable(0x7fff) returns false, not null.
  • src/protocol/InputCodec.js quantizeInput truncates expr instead of rounding it: it clamps to 0..255, then setUint8 drops the fraction (3.7 sends 3, 254.9 sends 254) with no case reported. Existing clients decode those bytes, so keep the truncation.
  • scripts/expr-truncation-witness.mjs pins that truncation and the expr clamp, and runs in the npm run check gate.
  • Wire v3 player record: 8 fields in a 22-byte bin; [inputSequence, inputBuffer, groundNormal] ride a per-recipient me block, so snapshots pack per recipient. getSnapshot() keys on _version === _snapshotVersion alone.
  • PhysicsNetworkClient.connect() REJECTS with a named TransportConnectError, never connected:false; retry belongs in the caller (_doReconnect). err.reason is stable: websocket-unavailable, websocket-error, websocket-closed-before-open, connect-superseded.
  • With prediction off, BaseClient keeps the raw input in the redundancy buffer and skips the quantized encode and decode per input step, which was the largest per-frame allocation the profiler named. The packets are byte-identical either way: a field the raw input omits encodes as the zero the template supplies. Non-finite and clamped fields are still refused and reported by the same encode the prediction path uses.
  • A socket becomes a player only through MIGRATE, RECONNECT, or silence past the MIGRATE-peek grace (ServerHandlers.js onClientConnect: a 50 ms floor divided by tick dilation, capped at 1500 ms), so a silent probe joins as a phantom player. Probes connect with ?probe=1, which ServerAPI.js turns into transport.probeOnly. Never answer an unresolvable RECONNECT with INVALID_SESSION and leave it open: rejectSession flushes and closes.
  • src/netcode/LockstepGameLoop.js throws at construction when inputDelayTicks exceeds INPUT_RETAIN_TICKS (240), and ignores inputs more than that many ticks ahead of the sim tick (futureInputsIgnored).
  • Headless Node witnesses that construct PhysicsNetworkClient must assign globalThis.WebSocket from ws first (see scripts/chart-reanchor-wire-harness.mjs, scripts/planet-multiplayer-harness.mjs); scripts/lib/witness-audit.mjs flags a WebSocket reach that lacks it.
  • src/netcode/LockstepGameLoop.js refuses an inputDelayTicks above INPUT_RETAIN_TICKS (240) at construction, because a peer can only simulate a tick once every peer's input for it is retained.
  • src/sdk/TickHandler.js processPlayerMovement consumes one input per tick, then catches up extra inputs only while inputs.length > INPUT_BUFFER_CATCHUP_DEPTH (8), capped by MAX_CATCHUP_STEPS_PER_TICK and the player's inputStepBudget.
  • src/netcode/RollbackGameLoop.js stalls (runs no simulation step) when the tick it would run is more than maxRollbackTicks (12 by default) ahead of the slowest remote's contiguous confirmed input (minRemoteConfirmed), so rollback depth, not rate, is the bounded quantity to measure. Construction also refuses maxRollbackTicks + inputDelayTicks at or above the state ring window.

Patched dependencies

  • scripts/patch-deps.mjs patch: src.includes(marker) is the only idempotency test, so each payload carries its own marker as a string constant (const _spointPatch… = "spoint patch] …"). A payload without its marker is injected again on every postinstall. scripts/patch-deps-marker-witness.mjs pins the 18 markers and simulates each file's injection sequence.
  • three.ez instanceIndex decl guard: a shader that includes neither #include <batching_pars_vertex> nor #include <instanced_pars_vertex> has no instanceIndex declaration, so the patch prepends _ezDecl. In the three.ez build batching_pars_vertex concatenates #include <instanced_pars_vertex>, so both chunks count as a declaration. Repo shaders that include instanced_pars_vertex directly: client/core/GrassMaterial.js, client/core/SSAO.js, client/core/WeatherMaterials.js.
  • three.ez bindTextures: the patch no longer reads gl.CURRENT_PROGRAM and restores it. WebGLState.useProgram in node_modules/three/build/three.module.js returns early when the program is unchanged, setProgram calls state.useProgram(program.program), and three.module.js reads CURRENT_PROGRAM nowhere.
  • three.ez BVH LOD hysteresis: the level distances passed to frustumCullingLOD shrink by each level's own hysteresis, distance - distance * hysteresis, the same levelDistance that getObjectLODIndexForDistance uses in node_modules/@three.ez/instanced-mesh/build/index.js.
  • msgpackr -0 escapes: -0 >>> 0 === 0 and -0 >> 0 === 0 both hold in JavaScript, so -0 enters the positive and negative fixint branches. The payload adds !Object.is(value, -0) to both tests in node_modules/msgpackr/pack.js and node_modules/msgpackr/dist/node.cjs.
  • The three.webgpu.js build (node_modules/three/build/three.webgpu.js) drops pending popErrorScope() rejections at 3 sites; scripts/patch-deps.mjs patches all three, and scripts/patch-deps-marker-witness.mjs pins their markers.

Hot swap

A module that calls globalThis.__spointHmr?.acceptSelf?.(import.meta.url, swap) accepts its own edits. A save reloads only that module, and swap(next) re-assigns each export to the freshly imported copy. Re-assigning is what makes the edit visible: an ESM import is a live binding, so importers already holding the old copy read the new code on their next call. Self-acceptance therefore costs one module fetch per save, not a page load. It re-points code only and does not rebuild state the page already built. Walking up to an ancestor acceptor cannot help, because a re-import is a new module instance that existing importers never read.

Dev HMR bodies

  • A ?hmr= body is a pure function of the file's bytes, the version floor, and the active version of the module and each of its direct imports. rewrite() stamps an import only while that import's version is above the floor, so a validator must cover exactly those. A body is re-sent only when one of them moves. Cache-Control: no-cache forces a revalidate before every use, so a reload answers 304 and does not re-read or re-rewrite the source.

spoint — README

spoint

Physics and netcode SDK for authoritative-server multiplayer games. Jolt physics, hot reload, app-based world logic, Three.js client.

Demo: https://anentrypoint.github.io/spoint/

Quick Start

npx spoint
# or
npm install && npm start
# open http://localhost:3002              -> multiplayer (WebSocket to this server)
# open http://localhost:3002/?singleplayer -> in-browser server (runs the same server in a Worker)

There is one server. npm start runs a single node server.js on one port (worldDef.port, default 3002). Multiplayer is /; the in-browser / singleplayer mode is /?singleplayer on the same origin — the client (client/app.js) picks a WebSocket client vs an in-Worker BrowserServer from the query string, not a second port. Do not run a second node server.js on a different PORT for the same project: each process freezes its own node_modules/asset snapshot at boot, so two instances drift (the ":3002 and a script-default :8090 look different" trap). Dev/eval scripts that default to PORT=8090 just start that one server on another port; use one instance and /?singleplayer instead. The server refuses a second instance on an in-use port with a clear message.

Creating Apps

node ./bin/create-app.js my-app

Or manually create apps/<name>/index.js:

export default {
  server: {
    setup(ctx) {
      ctx.state.counter = ctx.state.counter || 0
    },
    update(ctx, dt) {
      ctx.state.counter += dt
    },
    onInteract(ctx, player) { },
    onCollision(ctx, other) { }
  },
  client: {
    setup(engine) { },
    render(ctx) {
      return { custom: { mesh: 'box', color: 0xff0000, sx: 1, sy: 1, sz: 1 } }
    }
  }
}

World Config (apps/world/index.js)

export default {
  port: 3002,
  tickRate: 64,
  gravity: [0, -18, 0],
  movement: { maxSpeed: 14, sprintSpeed: 24, groundAccel: 300, airAccel: 30, friction: 5, stopSpeed: 1, jumpImpulse: 5.5 },
  player: { health: 100, capsuleRadius: 0.28, capsuleHalfHeight: 0.63, modelScale: 1.323, feetOffset: 0.027 },
  scene: { skyColor: 0x54c5eb, sunColor: 0xffffff, sunIntensity: 1.5, sunPosition: [21, 50, 20] },
  camera: { fov: 70, shoulderOffset: 0.35, zoomStages: [0, 1.5, 3, 5, 8], defaultZoomIndex: 2 },
  entities: [
    { id: 'env', model: './apps/maps/mymap.glb', position: [0, 0, 0], scale: [1, 1, 1], bodyType: 'static' },
    { id: 'game', position: [0, 0, 0], app: 'tps-game' }
  ],
  playerModel: '/assets/default-avatar.vrm',
  spawnPoint: [0, 2, 0]
}

Server-Side ctx API

PropertyDescription
ctx.statePersistent state (survives hot reload)
ctx.entity.id, .position, .rotation, .scale, .velocity, .custom, .destroy()
ctx.physics.addBoxCollider(), .addSphereCollider(), .addCapsuleCollider(), .addTrimeshCollider(), .addConvexFromModel()
ctx.interactable({ prompt, radius })Register E-key interaction
ctx.world.spawn(id, cfg), .destroy(id), .getEntity(id), .query(filter)
ctx.players.getAll(), .send(pid, msg), .broadcast(msg), .setPosition(pid, pos)
ctx.time.tick, .deltaTime, .elapsed, .after(sec, fn), .every(sec, fn)
ctx.bus.on(channel, fn), .emit(channel, data) — wildcard * suffix supported
ctx.lagCompensator.getPlayerStateAtTime(pid, ms) for hit detection
ctx.raycast(origin, dir, maxDist)Physics raycast

Client Engine API

PropertyDescription
engine.sceneTHREE.Scene
engine.cameraTHREE.PerspectiveCamera
engine.rendererTHREE.WebGLRenderer or WebGPURenderer
engine.THREEThree.js module
engine.clientNetwork client (PhysicsNetworkClient for multiplayer, or in-Worker BrowserServer for ?singleplayer)
engine.playerIdLocal player ID
engine.camCamera controller
engine.players.getMesh(id), .getState(id), .getAnimator(id)

Editor (in-browser)

Press P to toggle editor mode:

  • G / R / S — translate / rotate / scale gizmo
  • F — focus camera on selected entity
  • Del — delete selected entity
  • Drag-and-drop .glb files to place models

Singleplayer Mode

Add ?singleplayer to the URL to run the same server inside a Web Worker (BrowserServer + src/sdk/WorkerEntry.js) instead of connecting to it over WebSocket — same origin, same code, no separate process. ?world=<name> selects the world (default tps-game); a world module that fails to load stops the boot with a console error instead of substituting another world.

Architecture

server.js                         Entry point
src/sdk/server.js                 Creates subsystems
src/sdk/TickHandler.js            Per-tick: movement → physics → collisions → apps → snapshot
src/physics/World.js              Jolt physics wrapper
src/netcode/PhysicsIntegration.js CharacterVirtual per player
src/apps/AppRuntime.js            Entity system, app lifecycle
client/app.js                     Three.js renderer, VRM loading, input loop
client/BrowserServer.js           Runs the server in a Web Worker for ?singleplayer (same origin)

Production Build

npm run build:client

Bundles client/app.js and its static relative-import graph (client/core/, client/hud/, etc.) into a single minified dist/client/app.js via esbuild (scripts/bundle-client.mjs). Third-party packages that rely on import.meta.url-relative asset loading (three, mapspinner, jolt-physics, streaming-gltf's worker/loader siblings) and runtime-determined dynamic import()s (per-world app modules) are left external, not inlined.

buildStaticDirs (src/sdk/server.js) mounts dist/client/ at the same / prefix as the raw client/ dir, listed first — StaticHandler's per-mount fallthrough serves the bundled dist/client/app.js in place of the raw client/app.js automatically whenever the bundle exists on disk, and falls back to raw ESM (no-cache, hot-reload-friendly) otherwise. No env var or flag needed: build once (npm run build:client) before deploying, or skip it entirely for local dev.

Publishing to itch.io / GitHub Pages (no backend server)

node scripts/static-export.mjs dist-static

Assembles a fully static, backend-less export (bundled client + bundled Worker-side server code + the exact node_modules/apps subset needed) ready for itch.io's HTML5 upload or a GitHub Pages branch — see [docs/publish.md](docs/publish.md) for the full recipe, base-path handling for project-site subpaths, and verification steps.

Load Testing

npm run bots

Env vars: BOT_COUNT, BOT_DURATION, BOT_HZ, BOT_URL

Documentation

  • Getting Started: docs/getting-started.md — Full tutorial, world/app creation, API reference
  • Quick Reference: docs/quick-reference.md — Cheatsheet for config, physics, client, network messages
  • Architecture: docs/architecture-decisions.md
  • Rendering: docs/rendering.md
  • Editor: docs/editor-composing.md
  • Publishing: docs/publish.md
  • Anticheat: docs/anticheat.md

License

MIT

spoint — docs/FEATURES-full.md

spoint feature list

Every feature the spoint verifier inventory found, one line each, with its implementation path, its proof path and its status. This is the reference for jayzee's IDEAL.md: jayzee's IDEAL.md should cite these rows by feature name and proof path. The committed 176-row table at docs/FEATURES.md is a separate, earlier view and is not superseded by this file.

Generated from a read-only inventory at HEAD on 2026-11-08. Each row was produced by one agent per source area and then checked by a second agent that ran git ls-files and git grep on every path it kept.

Status

  • PROVEN: a tracked witness under scripts/ names the feature's symbol or string AND prints a RESULT: line. This means the feature is exercised by a witness. It does not mean every branch of the feature is covered; rows the verifier narrowed are marked with a note.
  • UNPROVEN: no tracked witness references the feature. The feature exists in code but nothing in the repo asserts it.
  • HELD: an owner decision not yet made. No row carries this status.

Totals: 1067 features, 223 PROVEN, 844 UNPROVEN.

Notes marked Verifier note are corrections or partial-coverage caveats that the verifier attached to a row.

spoint — docs/FEATURES.md

spoint features: netcode, protocol, client, sharding, static, animation

Scope: src/netcode, src/protocol, src/client, src/sharding, src/static, src/animation (rows 1-103) and packages/mapspinner (all of src/ and the top-level planet files, rows 104-176). Other source areas are not listed here yet.

Status rules: PROVEN means a tracked scripts/ witness references the feature's symbol and prints its own RESULT line from assertions. UNPROVEN means no such witness: either none references it, or the only reference is a path inventory (scripts/cross-map-parity.mjs), or the referencing script prints no RESULT line. HELD means an owner decision is pending.

Proof paths are repo-relative tracked files. A dash means no proof path.

A replay's growth is its last-entry offset minus the acknowledged error (growthM). Each replay also records the server groundY (server y when grounded or vertically blocked), the predicted groundY at the ack, and both gap. Verdict: PASS only if no replay grows more than 1 cm (the documented pop threshold, detectPops default) across at least 15 runs of the 50/10/2 on arm. The control injects a 0.03 m y error on every ack on the flat arena and must show growth over 1 cm; by code reading this may fail, since the finite sampler absorbs the error.Phase A, this commitscripts/groundy-replay-witness.mjs (driver), scripts/lib/groundy-replay-hook.mjs (preloaded with node --import, wraps onServerSnapshot and _rebaseAndReplay without editing the engine)Phase B run, final code (node scripts/groundy-replay-witness.mjs, 15 runs of 50/10/2 on): RESULT: FAIL (exit 1). 68 of 98 mover replays grew over 1 cm, in 15 of 15 runs, max growth 2.5917 m. None of those 68 had a groundY gap (serverGroundY equal to predictedGroundY), so the base mismatch is not what produced these replays. Local pops appeared in 8 of 15 runs, three of them at 23.99 pops/min, so the outlier reproduced in this harness. Control (node scripts/groundy-replay-witness.mjs --control): RESULT: PASS (exit 0). 66 mover replays carried the injected 0.03 m groundY gap and 42 of them grew over 1 cm, so the mechanism does produce growth when the base is mismatched. Those 66 are every replay that still carries the gap, not only the injected acks. Corrections to the Phase A note: the harness installs no ground provider (predictionGroundSurface is never set), so characterStep takes its floor from state.groundY on every step, and the flat-ground absorption argument did not hold. No documented replay-offset threshold exists; 1 cm is taken from the pop definition. Phase B fixes were to the witness only: the hook keyed engines wrongly (WeakMap has no size), so the pooled shooter and bots were counted and the mover was not isolated; the mover is now matched to the harness's own correction count and jump statistics; the injection was applied on every ack, which the base absorbs, so it is now every 25th local ack; and the output now counts over-limit replays that carry a groundY gap. Open, owner decision: what causes the natural replay growth if not the groundY base, and whether the sampler-edge world is still needed to test the base. The replay base is unchanged.

Decisions and open items, no code change

If it is ever wired in, it needs a witness that compares it with a fresh replay.
  • Row 2, applyChartTransfer (src/client/PredictionEngine.js): reviewed, no change. Of the 20 non-first-contact corrections with a jump above 0.2 m, all had ack errors of 0.03 to 0.23 m, a grounded flat walk, verticallyBlocked false and stalled 0, and the per-step replay offsets grew from 0.03 m to 1.02 m over nine inputs. The replay base is not changed. A change needs a witness that puts a grounded ack 3 to 30 cm off the predicted path on flat ground and checks that the replay matches the live path step by step.
  • Row 21, probeSpawnGroundY (src/sdk/Relocation.js): drops the reason and returns only y. Its four callers (lines 101, 114, 120, 221) treat null as groundless and do not need the reason, so no change. The reason-carrying path is probeSpawnHoldGround.
  • Row 21, silent default: SessionStore uses options.ttl || 30000 and server.js uses config.sessionTTL || 60000, so a TTL of 0 (or NaN) becomes the default. Whether 0 is a valid TTL is an owner decision. Not changed.
